| T7130 | [Reel aspect ratio (9:16 / 16:9) cannot be changed on a phone](tasks/T7130-mobile-aspect-ratio-toggle.md) | 6 | 2 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-18 prod). Bugs **41p** (primary) + **42p** (duplicate, linked 2026-08-17), both from arshia on build 23eda79d. **Not an iOS/Safari issue** despite the reports — reproduced in desktop Chrome by narrowing the window. The interactive selector sat in a `hidden lg:flex` controls bar (`FramingModeView.jsx:352`), so it vanished below 1024px (iPads in portrait too), and what mobile got instead was `<AspectRatioSelector readOnly />` (T5780): a div styled like the control whose only "not tappable" signal is a `title` tooltip touch never surfaces — so users tapped it forever and reported a broken button. Ratio was therefore only choosable at reel-creation time; auto-generated reels default to 9:16 and a phone-only user could never reshape one. Fix: one controls bar at every width holding the one real selector (dim/straighten/zoom stay desktop-only), 44px touch targets with a `lg:` reset so desktop geometry is byte-identical, read-only look-alike deleted (zero call sites after this, and the fake-control shape IS the bug). Verified in a real browser at 376px (tap 16:9 → persisted, tap back → restored) and 1280px; touch sizing re-verified on emulated iPhone 14, **iPad landscape (1080px wide but coarse-pointer — the case a `lg:` breakpoint misses)** and fine-pointer desktop. Aspect buttons added to the `framing` usability manifest (`actions` + `touchTargets`) so this control class is guarded in CI, not just ad hoc. |
| T7150 | [Collection public-share link freezes "no intro" before the intro picker is used](tasks/T7150-collection-share-intro-sequencing.md) | 3 | 2 | - | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-18 prod). Bug **43p**: shared collection link showed no intro card. Confirmed the share froze `intro_card_id: 0` at creation. `CollectionShareModal.jsx`'s public-link toggle (`handleTogglePublic`) creates the share **immediately** on flip, using whatever `introCardId` happens to be at that instant (default `null` → freezes to 0) — but the Intro picker is rendered *below* the toggle, so the natural flow creates the link before the user reaches the picker. Once created there's no update path (deliberately — matches the reel-share behavior). Fix: move the Intro picker above the toggle, and defer public-link creation to an explicit action so the freeze genuinely happens after the intro choice, not before it. Verified live on staging with a real dev-login'd share + presigned unfurl check (facebookexternalhit/Googlebot/Twitterbot all 200 with correct og: tags). |
| T7180 | [Overlay highlight region key-format mismatch drops the spotlight silently](tasks/T7180-overlay-region-key-mismatch.md) | 7 | 3 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-18 prod). Bug **44p**: arshia — spotlight doesn't appear after export once the highlight region is dragged to the middle of a clip. Root-caused via expert agent + live read-only check of prod project 96's DB: the stored region carries BOTH `startTime`/`endTime` (camelCase, where the user dragged it, `[2.45,4.18]`) and `start_time`/`end_time` (snake_case, the original auto-generated default, `[0.0,2.0]`) with genuinely different values. `update_region` writes camelCase; the auto-region generator writes snake_case; every reader (render-path `_region_bounds`, frontend `restoreRegions`) prefers snake_case when both are present — so a lever drag visually moves the bar but the persisted/rendered bounds never leave the auto default, silently dropping every manually-placed keyframe outside `[0,2]`. Export completes 200/"complete" with no error. Compounding: the lever-drag pointermove handler fires an unthrottled network POST per frame of the drag (~200 in the reported session, confirmed via console logs), inflating `overlay_version` to 2545 on one video. Fix: canonicalize writes on snake_case in `overlay.py`, commit-on-release instead of per-pointermove in `RegionLayer.jsx`/`OverlayScreen.jsx`. No schema impact. User tested locally and approved before merge. |
| T7650 | [Bug 45p: "Top Plays" locked meter reads as broken](tasks/T7650-top-plays-locked-ui-confusion.md) | 3 | 2 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-26 prod). arshia (2026-08-21): locked Top Plays meter shows for the game but not the clip, sometimes resolving after a wait. | T7190 | [Quest panel occludes home-screen content](tasks/T7190-quest-panel-occludes-home-content.md) | 4 | 1 | 4.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-19 prod). Root cause: the home-screen `inline` (`relative`-positioned) render path still applied the fixed-overlay `positionStyle` offsets, and `relative` (unlike `static`) honors them. | T7200 | [Admin loses admin status after impersonation reload; move Admin entry into profile switcher](tasks/T7200-admin-profile-switcher-and-reload-bug.md) | 5 | 3 | 1.7 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-19 prod). User-reported 2026-08-18: `authStore.checkAdmin()` is never called on a hard reload (`setSessionState` skips it whenever `skipFetches=true`, which `initSession()` always passes — the "bootstrap provides admin data" comment is false, `/api/bootstrap` never returns `is_admin`), so impersonate/stop-impersonate (both hard-reload) silently drop admin access. Also folds in a UI request: the floating `AdminButton` (mis-labeled "fixed top-right" but missing the actual fixed classes, so it renders at the page bottom) moves into the `ManageProfilesModal` profile-switcher list as a gated "Admin" row instead of a separate floating control. |
| T7210 | [Export slowness diagnostics + Modal call_id recovery bug](tasks/T7210-export-slowness-diagnostics-modal-call-id.md) | 5 | 2 | 2.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-19 prod). User-reported 2026-08-19: a framing export looked stuck; live prod+Modal investigation found it was a genuine slow render (`gpu_seconds=1000.45` vs. the ~334s E6 benchmark anchor), not a hang, but diagnosing that required manual cross-referencing across Postgres/SQLite/Modal logs with no direct signal. Also found `export_jobs.modal_call_id` never populates for any Modal generator export — `modal_client.py`'s `hasattr(gen, 'object_id')` check is dead code under the installed SDK (1.3.1), `remote_gen()` never exposes that attribute. Fixed (expert-verified) via `modal.current_function_call_id()` emitted as the first stream item; reviewer caught 2 blocking issues in the first pass (GeneratorDone-as-success could finalize a row pointing at a missing R2 object; making recovery actually work opened a new in-band-vs-recovery double-finalize race) — both fixed (R2 existence check, CAS claim on `export_jobs.stage`) and covered by new regression tests. Adds per-checkpoint elapsed/throughput logging (Modal + backend). Modal deployed, merged to master 2026-08-19. |
| T7220 | [Multi-clip temp-source cleanup awaited a sync function](tasks/T7220-multiclip-temp-cleanup-await-bug.md) | 2 | 1 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-19 prod). Found live in staging logs while verifying T7210: `multi_clip.py` awaited `delete_from_r2` (a plain sync function everywhere else in the codebase), throwing `TypeError` on its bool return every export and logging a false "failed to delete" even though the R2 delete had already succeeded. Existing tests mocked it as `async` specifically to mask the mismatch. Fixed with `asyncio.to_thread` (matching the pattern used a few lines earlier for `upload_bytes_to_r2`); test mocks corrected to match the real sync contract; added a regression assertion verified to fail on the reverted code. |

| T8050 | [Focus mode shows "No video loaded" instead of a loading state](tasks/T8050-focus-no-video-loaded-flash.md) | 5 | 2 | 2.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). RESOLVED 2026-08-30: found live-testing T8040. Root cause: the annotate-leaving invalidateClips call (App.jsx) never set projectDataStore.isLoading -- only the unread clipsFetching flag -- so FocusScreen's isProjectLoading stayed false for the whole clips-fetch window and VideoPlayer fell through to its empty "No video loaded" state instead of the spinner. Fixed by bracketing both invalidateClips call sites with setLoading(true,'clips')/setLoading(false), mirroring useProjectLoader's existing pattern -- reuses the existing "Loading clips..." message, no new UI. Verified live via a mocked slow fetch: captured "Loading clips..." where "No video loaded" used to render. Merged to master (PR [#311](https://github.com/imankha/video-editor/pull/311), squash, CI green). |

| T7940 | [Game poster cache leaks one user's thumbnail onto another user's game tile](tasks/T7940-game-poster-cross-account-leak.md) | 9 | 3 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). RESOLVED 2026-08-28: all 3 poster routes (games/projects/downloads) now take a `profile_id` query param the frontend sets from the owner's current profile; backend refuses a mismatched token with 403 before any DB/R2 read (cache-correctness token, not the auth mechanism — real scoping via X-Profile-ID header was already correct). 9 unit tests pin the guard's internal logic per route + 3 real-HTTP-route integration tests (real TestClient, real per-profile SQLite, two accounts both with game id=1 mirroring the actual prod incident) prove it end-to-end; counterfactually verified both suites fail if the guard is removed. Merged to master (PR #306, CI green). Two prod-only verification steps remain (CDN cf-cache-status check, live mikhail.k.taylor@gmail.com verification) — need dashboard/prod access, not available to this session. Confirmed live in PROD 2026-08-27: mikhail.k.taylor@gmail.com's game tile ("Vs Toronto JR Argos May 15") shows imankh@gmail.com's video frame as its thumbnail — a cross-account private-media leak. Root cause: `/api/games/{id}/poster.jpg` keys purely on the per-profile SQLite AUTOINCREMENT game id (nearly every account has a game id=1) with `Cache-Control: private` but no per-user/profile token in the URL and no `Vary` — a URL-keyed cache (most likely Cloudflare edge in front of the API, or a shared proxy) can serve one user's cached bytes to another's identical-looking request. Confirmed via prod read-only DB comparison: both accounts' id=1 games have DIFFERENT blake3_hash (`d971fd15...` vs `043f62e2...`), ruling out a real video-dedup collision — this is purely a cache-key defect. Same pattern exists on `/api/projects/{id}/poster.jpg` and `/api/downloads/{id}/poster.jpg`. Fix: make poster URLs unique per (user, profile), not just per numeric id; server must still independently verify session ownership (URL token is cache-correctness only, never authorization). |

| T7950 | [Root-cause the double-UploadId multipart leak (2/2 recurrence)](tasks/T7950-double-uploadid-multipart-leak.md) | 5 | 4 | 1.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). RESOLVED 2026-08-28: expert-confirmed compound mechanism — H1 a non-idempotent CreateMultipartUpload retried by retry_r2_call could mint a second live multipart on ack-loss; H3 the fresh-create path's orphan-abort ran unscoped under a per-user lock that doesn't cover the global R2 key, letting a cross-user prepare abort another's just-stored UploadId. Fixed: dropped the create retry in favor of list-and-adopt on ambiguous/transient failure (classified via existing is_transient_error, not a blanket catch), reordered create-before-scoped-abort using the already-existing keep_upload_id param. Cross-machine lock (Option A) explicitly deferred, only if a future sweep shows recurrence. 4 regression tests (RED pre-fix, GREEN post-fix) + 70 related tests green. Merged to master (PR #303, CI green). |
| T7910 | [Signup attribution: referrer fallback + pipeline verification](tasks/T7910-signup-referrer-attribution.md) | 8 | 3 | 2.7 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). From the 2026-08-27 refresh: 14 signups in 3 days (= the prior 3 months), ALL origin=organic with NULL UTMs — the only growth spike on record is unattributable. Capture gap, not just absence: App.jsx:139-171 never captures document.referrer, and the landing->app hop destroys the original referrer (PageLayout relays utm/ref but not its own referrer). Fix: landing relays lref=<host>, app stores the hint, _determine_origin gains a lowest-priority referrer branch (seo/social/community; hostname only, no new PG columns — populates the EXISTING origin field). Shared host->bucket constant that investor-analytics T7410 reuses. Step 1 is a cheap staging end-to-end UTM verification (nobody has ever proven the pipeline works). |

| T4310 | ↳ [R2 Version-Conflict Detection (CAS) — upload side](tasks/durability-sync/T4310-r2-version-conflict-detection.md) | 9 | 5 | 1.8 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). `skip_version_check=True` at EVERY R2 upload site = last-write-wins on whole profile DBs. Reinstate as CAS (background/worker syncs first); conflicts loud (freeze + escalate), never silent. Archaeology first: why was it bypassed. Must not regress T2720. **Proven single-server 2026-07-24** (move_reels stale writer vs R2's newer state). Interlocks with T4315. Architect gate. |
| T4315 | ↳ [Restore-on-staleness — local copy not authoritative forever](tasks/durability-sync/T4315-local-authoritative-restore-staleness.md) | 8 | 4 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). RESTORE-side sibling of T4310. `ensure_user_database` restores `user.sqlite` only when `local_version is None`, so a machine's local copy is authoritative for its lifetime + out-of-band repair is impossible while a session is live — that + middleware-syncs-session-user-only is how the 400-credit grant died on a deploy. Add restore-if-newer for `user.sqlite` write paths; generalize move_reels' `require_fresh` to all writers (a5ff3e48 point-fix); never `_ensure_empty_profile_db` after an R2 ERROR. CAS (T4310) + restore-if-newer (T4315) interlock. |

| T6345 | [postgres migration runner skips version gaps permanently (max vs set membership)](tasks/T6345-postgres-migration-runner-skips-version-gaps.md) | 8 | 2 | 4.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-13 prod). **Moved ahead of T5140 (user-ordered 2026-08-10)** — was in Milestone A, not UI-visible, but the user chose to land it before the reshoot. **Sibling defect filed out of T6340 (2026-08-02); a separate runner bug, its own regression test.** `MigrationRunner.get_pending` (`app/migrations/base.py:38`) computes pending as `version > MAX(applied)`. For postgres, `get_current_version` is `SELECT MAX(version) FROM schema_migrations` - a maximum, NOT a set-membership test - so a migration numbered BELOW an already-applied version is skipped SILENTLY and permanently, no error, no log. It really happened: T5770 correctly numbered its migration v022 (v020/v021 reserved by then-unmerged Share the Game branches) and merged first, leaving dev + staging at `applied: [1..19, 22], GAPS: [20, 21]` while `run_all_migrations()` reported success. `shares.share_type` never gained `'game_link'`, so every public-game-link creation would 500 on the CHECK constraint on an env reporting itself fully migrated. Fixed by hand on dev + staging (both now contiguous 1-22); the runner defect is untouched and will recur the next time branches merge out of numeric order - which the branch-numbering discipline actively encourages. FIX: compare against the applied SET (`SELECT version FROM schema_migrations`), not the max. Regression test: seed `[1..19, 22]`, assert v020/v021 still pending. Also assess whether the `PRAGMA user_version` tracks (user_db/profile_db) can gap the same way - they hold a single integer so they only move forward, but a merge landing a lower version has the same silent-skip shape. **Shipped 2026-08-10 via /dotask container:** implemented exactly as scoped, `PRAGMA user_version` tracks assessed and documented as not needing the fix (single-integer, can't express a gap on disk). Merged to master (PR #242, `37e7b49a`). |
| T6410 | [migration swap discards unsynced local writes - and T6340 now publishes the result](tasks/T6410-migration-swap-discards-unsynced-writes.md) | 6 | 3 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-13 prod). **Moved ahead of T5140 (user-ordered 2026-08-10)** — was in Milestone A, not UI-visible, but the user chose to land it before the reshoot. **Filed 2026-08-03 from T6340 review (out-of-scope note).** `_migrate_profile_db` decides swap-vs-keep by SCHEMA version (`PRAGMA user_version`), which says nothing about data recency - a local copy with unsynced writes (pending/failed upload, `.sync_pending` set) usually has the SAME schema version as R2, so the else-branch swaps R2 bytes over it and discards the pending writes. Pre-existing, but T6340 raised the stakes: the swap now records a confirmed baseline and uploads at r2_version+1, making the discard canonical (before, CAS refused and the loss stayed local). FIX: refuse the profile (distinct status, mirror of wal_busy) when a pending-sync signal is present; never merge, let the retry flush first. Test: seed unsynced local writes + pending marker, assert writes survive and nothing uploads. **Shipped 2026-08-10 via /dotask container, expert-consulted: the `.sync_pending`-marker refusal design above was found WRONG** (per-user not per-profile scope, never cleared on success -> would permanently false-refuse). Real fix gates the swap on the SYNC baseline instead — skip the swap and migrate local in place when `local_baseline >= downloaded_sync_version` (R2 has nothing local lacks), reporting `ok`, not a new refusal status. Merged to master (PR #245, `95b805fa`). |

| T7090 | [Intro cards silently drop on OOM — move download-time compose to Modal](tasks/T7090-intro-card-oom-modal-dispatch.md) | 8 | 7 | 1.1 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-16 prod). Modal function `compose_serve_time_modal` deployed same day. Filed 2026-08-16 from live staging testing during the T4947/T6240/T6760/T6360 promotion check. A reel with an intro card downloaded successfully (200 OK) but silently shipped WITHOUT the intro — playback showed it fine, only the download path burns it into video. Root-caused via live log + reproduction on a matched-memory Docker image: the intro-card ffmpeg graph feeds one looped full-frame PNG input PER visual layer (~150MB peak RSS each, independent of clip length — a 20s reel triggered this), extrapolating to ~1.7GB demanded on the 1GB Fly machine (staging AND production run the identical spec) — kernel OOM-kills ffmpeg mid-render, and the SIGKILL is silently swallowed into an ordinary "no intro" case with a 200 response. Same pattern as T7020: user decided download-time compose (intro/outro/T6360 metadata) should move to Modal, not run local ffmpeg on the Fly web machine — but unlike T7020's upload case, a download must wait for bytes back, so the Architect resolved real sync-latency-vs-OOM-risk tradeoffs rather than porting T7020's fire-and-forget shape. Also fixed regardless of infra: collapsed the intro card's static PIL layers + cropped text layers to their bbox (cuts ~1.1GB of the waste on its own), and stopped swallowing SIGKILL into a silent degrade (CRITICAL log + surfaced signal, mirrors T4947/T6360's `report[full_fidelity]` pattern). |

| T6380 | ["Remove" on a custom cover is a no-op, and the uploaded state is never read back](tasks/T6380-poster-remove-upload-is-a-noop.md) | 6 | 3 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **Found 2026-08-02 while scoping T5410's documented limitation - there are TWO bugs, not one.** (1) `wrappedRemoveUpload` (`OverlayScreen.jsx:908-910`) is `setPosterUploadedFilename(null)` and **makes no backend call at all**, so the uploaded image stays in R2 under the deterministic poster key and every consumer (`shares.py::_resolve_poster`, edge og:image, share email) keeps serving the cover the user just removed; `poster_source` stays `'upload'`, so `backfill_posters` skips the reel even under `force` and it never self-heals - only a full re-export overwrites it. (2) NOT in T5410's note: `posterUploadedFilename` is written ONLY from the upload response (`:897`) and **never hydrated from the backend**, so after a reload the panel shows the auto/marker copy and the marker renders active while R2 still holds the uploaded image - the "Custom image in use" state exists only in the session that uploaded. Net: the cover-photo UI is session-local and diverges from truth in BOTH directions. FIX: a real gesture-only `poster/revert` endpoint that **reuses `generate_poster_at_export`** (do not fork a second selection path - T5410 deliberately kept one) and resets `poster_source` to `'auto'`/`'overlay'`; plus add `poster_source` to `/overlay-data` **behind a `column_exists` guard** (v032 column on a hot read path - the T5630 `_has_stage_columns` landmine) and hydrate the uploaded state on load. Consumers need zero changes (same deterministic key). |
| T6390 | [The sync-conflict banner is undiagnosable - log the "why" on both client and server](tasks/T6390-sync-conflict-diagnostics.md) | 7 | 3 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **User direction 2026-08-02, triggered by a live staging incident that could NOT be root-caused.** User hit "Could not save to the cloud / A newer version of your work exists" in Annotate; neither side retained enough to explain it. CLIENT: `checkSyncStatus` (`syncStore.js:154`) flips state off the `X-Sync-Status` header and renders the banner with **zero console output** - the incident dump contains only page-load `[SLOW FETCH]` lines and does not even prove the conflict occurred. `retrySyncToR2` swallows every failure (`catch { return false }`, `:90`) and reloads on `restored` with no trace. SERVER: the `[SYNC_CONFLICT]` CRITICAL exists (`storage.py:1198` + the user.sqlite twin `:1471`) but `flyctl logs --no-tail` retains only ~100 lines (~90s of a chatty request log), so it had already scrolled out - the retained window held only the recovery (`Retry succeeded`, v2704 at 01:18:47). The `.sync_conflict` marker that DRIVES the banner outlives the log line but stores only `str(time.time())`, discarding the diagnosis exactly when it is written. Even read in time the line omits `req_id`/method/path - though the correlation already exists end-to-end (client mints `X-Request-ID` at `sessionInit.js:77,87`; server logs `req_id` on `[REQ]`/`[R2_CALL]`) - and omits **who moved R2 ahead**, since object metadata carries only `db-version` (`:1251`), leaving "another machine" / export worker / admin restore / migration indistinguishable. Also, `db_sync.py` logs `logger.error` for a FOREIGN db (`:1094,:1118`) but the session user's OWN conflict (`:1056`) returns the header silently. Preserve the one fact the line does pin: `loaded=None` (unconfirmed baseline, T6340/T4315 class) vs `loaded=vN` (stale, T6160 class) are different bugs with an identical banner. Staging runs ONE machine (`min_machines_running=0`, suspend), so a cross-machine race is NOT the explanation and the log should say so without a `flyctl machines list`. FIX (diagnostics only, **never weaken the CAS refusal, no fallback/auto-merge**): markers carry a JSON diag (reason/db/profile/loaded/r2/machine/req_id/method/path, tolerating the legacy float body); an `X-Sync-Diag` response header - **must be added to `expose_headers` (`main.py:217`) or it is invisible cross-origin on staging/prod**; a client `console.error` on the transition into conflict/failed plus retry-outcome logging; `req_id`+method+path in both CRITICALs and one boundary ERROR for the session's own conflict; and `db-writer`/`db-written-at` R2 metadata read back from the HEAD that **already runs** on the conflict path (zero extra R2 calls). Sync-layer twin of T6330. Do NOT add a per-request HEAD (T6160), never log presigned URLs (T6330), keep the success path silent (T2880/T3380). **PART B - a REAL defect found while scoping, fix it too:** `.sync_conflict`/`.sync_failed` are per-USER marker files (`database.py:88`) describing per-DB, per-PROFILE state, and every success path clears them unconditionally - so **a success on one DB erases a live conflict on another**. A `user.sqlite`-only request (`db_sync.py:1045-1051` -> `database.py:1596`) wipes a profile conflict, the header returns to `ok` and the banner vanishes **while the profile DB is still conflicted** - the user keeps editing on a DB whose writes are refused, with no signal (the silent-stale shape T6040 exists to prevent). Worse, `retry_pending_sync` stomps itself in ONE call: profile branch marks (`:375`), user.sqlite success branch clears (`:393`), so `_redrain_sync`'s bail-out `if has_sync_conflict(...)` (`:1211`) reads a cleared marker and **blind-retries a CAS conflict it explicitly documents as not blind-retryable (`:1191-93`) to exhaustion, then reports generic `failed`** - wrong banner, and Retry takes the blind re-upload path instead of restore-if-newer. The authors patched only the CONCURRENT branch (`:1016-1027` post-gather reassert, "one thread's clear can stomp the other's mark") and even that reasserts from THIS request's two statuses, clearing conflicts for DBs it never touched. Fix by SCOPING the marker to the DB/profile it describes (converges with Part 1's `db` diag field), not by adding more reassertions; `sync_status_header` reports a conflict if ANY scope is conflicted; keep the `conflict` > `failed` > `pending` priority. Failing test per stomp first (`bug-reproduction`); the `retry_pending_sync` self-stomp is deterministic and needs no concurrency. |
| T6402 | [A machine CAS-conflicts with itself - the baseline/HEAD decision runs outside the upload lock](tasks/T6402-cas-baseline-read-outside-upload-lock.md) | 8 | 3 | 2.7 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **Live staging incident 2026-08-03, root-caused in one pass by T6390's diagnostics on their first real use.** User clicked "Move to My Reels"; My Reels then took very long to load and the console showed `state -> conflict db=profile reason=stale_baseline loaded=2734 r2=2735 machine=d8933d5f417308 writer=d8933d5f417308/dcce51f3`. **`machine == writer machine` - the machine refused its own write.** Staging runs ONE machine (`min_machines_running=0`, suspend), so a cross-machine race is structurally impossible. ROOT CAUSE: in `sync_database_to_r2_with_version` the CAS decision is NOT covered by the lock that serialises the upload - baseline read (`database.py:1640`), HEAD (`storage.py:1211`) and the refuse branch (`:1246`) all run BEFORE `get_upload_lock` is acquired (`:1284/1297`). Two concurrent syncs of one profile interleave: A reads baseline 2734; B reads 2734, HEADs 2734, takes the lock, PUTs 2735, advances the baseline; A's HEAD then sees 2735 > 2734 and refuses. **Both upload the SAME file on disk**, so A's "stale" copy already contains B's data - the conflict is a false positive against itself. Concurrency per user is by design and documented at `db_sync.py:1282-84` ("fire-and-forget `_background_sync` tasks are not serialised per user"); T5870 round 2 gave the RE-DRAIN path a non-blocking lock probe but the PRIMARY sync path never got the equivalent guard for its version decision. The publish click alone fires the durable publish sync + `recordAchievement('moved_to_my_reels')` (`DraftTile.jsx:157`, fire-and-forget), with an export-worker sync possibly still draining. Same shape in the `user.sqlite` twin (`:1543` vs `:1569`). CONSEQUENCES: (1) false "edits aren't saving" banner; (2) the refusal calls `schedule_profile_db_reheal` (`database.py:644`), nulling the baseline so the next request re-downloads the ENTIRE profile.sqlite from R2 - that is the slow My Reels load; (3) a genuine narrow data-loss window - usually benign since the winner's PUT carries the loser's rows, but a row COMMITTED after the winner's PUT and before the loser's HEAD is refused and then DISCARDED by the re-heal (T6160 decision 2); here it was a quest achievement, on a keyframe `/actions` write it would be a real edit. FIX: move the baseline re-read + HEAD + `new_version` INSIDE the upload lock. Cross-machine CAS is untouched (R2 metadata stays authoritative) - it only stops a process racing itself. **Do NOT weaken the refusal, no fallback/auto-merge/force-push/blind-retry, do not advance the baseline on refusal (T4310/T4315/T6340 all exist because these were violated), no per-request HEAD (T6160), keep the success path silent (T2880/T3380).** Keep the `lock_timeout` bail-out ahead of the HEAD and re-verify T5920's `checkpoint_busy` still maps to FAILED not CONFLICT. Red-first test is deterministic (order the loser's HEAD after the winner's PUT); no concurrency flake needed. Incident logs already rotated out (~2.5 min retention, machine autosuspended), so the fix must be pinned by test, not log archaeology. |
| T6405 | [Branch CI runs both layers on every push, including the one the diff cannot reach](tasks/T6405-branch-ci-layer-scoping.md) | 5 | 2 | 2.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **User direction 2026-08-03 while reviewing T6402: "only run ci tests that may have actually been effected by the code change and not all tests."** `branch-ci.yml` ran BOTH jobs unconditionally on every push: frontend (npm ci + 4 lint/architecture gates + full vitest) and backend (postgres service + ffmpeg + pip install + ruff gates + full pytest). T6402's diff touched `src/backend/**` and docs only, yet the whole frontend job ran - measured 5m07s for the run. FIX: a `changes` job diffs vs master and gates the two existing jobs via `needs`/`if` - frontend skipped unless `src/frontend/**` or `scripts/**` changed (the frontend job runs check-viewport-units.mjs / check-media-api-base.mjs from there), backend skipped unless `src/backend/**` changed, and a `branch-ci.yml` edit runs BOTH so the workflow always re-validates itself. **JOB-level only - within a job the suite still runs IN FULL.** **Per-test-file selection was asked for, considered and REJECTED:** Python's import graph means a `storage.py` change is exercised by tests that never name storage (anything importing `app.main` - test_session_pinning, test_background_sync, test_export_worker_sync, test_t4050_durable_sync), so filename matching would silently skip real regressions and defer them past merge into Master CI; sound intra-suite selection needs coverage data (testmon), not path matching. Bias is toward RUNNING - a missed run is a silent regression, a redundant run costs only wall-clock. Master CI is UNCHANGED (still the full sweep on every merge). Reuses the existing `git diff --name-only origin/master...HEAD` pattern from the ESLint/Ruff steps - no third-party action. Safe today because master has NO branch protection (API 404), so a skipped job cannot wedge a required check - **re-verify if protection is ever added.** CLAUDE.md's Test Scope Policy claimed Branch CI "runs the complete vitest+pytest suites on every push"; that sentence is false under this change and is corrected in the same commit. |
| T6340 | [profile_db migrations can NEVER reach R2 - runner destroys its own sync baseline](tasks/T6340-profile-db-migrations-cannot-upload.md) | 9 | 4 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **Found 2026-08-02 running staging migrations after the T5770/T6310 merge. Affects PROD identically.** No profile_db migration can be applied on any R2-enabled deployment: the migration applies locally, the R2 upload is refused every time, and T6160 then discards the local change on next access. This is why **profile_db v030 (T5800) is still unapplied on staging** - profiles sit at schema 29 while master head is 30. Reproduced deterministically 4x; postgres migrated fine (v022), only profile_db fails. ROOT CAUSE: `_migrate_profile_db` force-downloads the canonical R2 copy and `shutil.move`s it over `db_path`, bypassing `ensure_database`. **The R2 object carries no `db_version` row** (the sync version lives in R2 object metadata), so the swap leaves the local file with no baseline; `get_local_db_version` returns None; the T4315 CAS guard's "unconfirmed baseline" branch (`storage.py:1197`) refuses unconditionally since R2 always has content. NOT a race - guaranteed. Pre-healing via `ensure_database` DOES restore the baseline (verified: None -> 3337 / 2697) but the very next run re-swaps the file in its own process and wipes it again: T4315 and T6160 deadlock, both behaving correctly. FIX: after the move, record the downloaded R2 sync version as the confirmed local baseline (memory cache + `db_version` row) so the later `sync_db_to_r2_explicit` sees no conflict. **Do NOT weaken the CAS guard** - T4315 exists because force-pushing an unconfirmed DB over real data caused prior data loss; fix the caller. Also thread the real `r2_version` into error rows (currently always null, which made a CAS refusal look like an R2 outage). Needs an integration test that fails on today's code, plus a prod audit for below-head profiles. Not data-loss: guards prevented the bad write, staging verified intact (game counts 6/2/10, local_sync == r2_sync after heal). **UPDATE 2026-08-02 (post Share the Game merge, master `43728f77`): reproduced a 5th time; the stuck list is now v030 (T5800) AND v031 (T5725), profile_db head is 31 not 30. NEW user-visible consequence — T5725 deliberately omitted a layer predicate on `_filter_clips_for_tag` because "the migration makes data correct"; with v031 unrunnable that premise is FALSE, so the legacy email-teammate share (clips.py:2566/2706/2748, still UI-reachable) can leak a My-Athlete clip to a teammate. The NEW epic share path is safe (`resolve_scoped_clips` intersects with the Team layer). Also filed inside the task: a SIBLING runner defect that deserves its own task — `get_pending` compares `version > MAX(applied)`, so a migration numbered below an already-applied one is skipped SILENTLY FOREVER; this really happened (dev+staging sat at `[1..19, 22]`, gaps 20/21, reporting "migrated" while `share_type` never gained `game_link`). Fixed by hand on both; the runner bug is untouched.** |
| T6190 | [Opening a reel refetches the drafts data before the editor loads](tasks/T6190-project-open-redundant-fetches.md) | 7 | 2 | 3.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **REGRESSION RESOLVED + MERGED 2026-07-31** (master `4cdcc3e8`). The render loop below was fixed on-branch by `cb84edc8` (stable save-handler registration); the live QA spec now passes against real data (4 passed / 1 data-skip): annotate->framing fires exactly 1 clips-list GET, 0 `/api/games`, 0 `/api/health` on SPA project-open, and a dragged boundary propagates (end_time 2991.5 both sides). Prior note kept for history: **REGRESSION ON BRANCH (2026-07-30) — DO NOT MERGE.** Branch pushed + CI green, but the user driving the real app hit `Maximum update depth exceeded` in `<FramingScreen>` on the annotate->framing transition (React kills the screen). Stack lands on `clearSaveCurrentClipState` (framingStore.js:45) in an effect cleanup; the register/clear effect at FramingScreen.jsx:396-399 keys on `[framingSaveCurrentClipState]`, whose `useCallback` (FramingContainer.jsx:301) depends on `clips`/`keyframes`/segment identities, and BOTH the body and cleanup setState. Suspected trigger: this task moved the clips fetch EARLIER (`invalidateClips` in App.handleModeChange) so it lands mid-mount instead of post-mount — log shows the video load abort+restart and `useSegments` restoring 3x, the last for a DIFFERENT clip (11.553s -> 9.992s). Under investigation; must bisect vs master before assuming it is ours. Original scope below. **TOP (user, 2026-07-28).** "Clicking a reel reloads the current view before loading framing/overlay" — confirmed in prod HAR. FramingScreen's mount effects refire `/api/games` (already hydrated by bootstrap) + a duplicate clips fetch (`_clipsInflight` latch already cleared; the "dedupe makes it free" comment at FramingScreen.jsx:476 is wrong — the calls are sequential, not concurrent), and `<ConnectionStatus />` mounting editor-only pays `/api/health` at navigation time. 3 wasted requests in front of `playback-url`; first video byte at 2.7s. Overlay is unaffected except for the health check. |
| T6200 | [Concurrent same-session API requests appear to serialize (~1.4s each, all finishing together)](tasks/T6200-concurrent-request-serialization.md) | 8 | 5 | 1.6 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **MERGED 2026-07-31** (master `4cdcc3e8`). Blocking request-path I/O offloaded to a bounded 32-thread executor + a `BoundedSemaphore` checkout gate sized to the PG pool (`_MAX_POOL_CONN=10`) so the new concurrency can't push `getconn` past `maxconn` into PoolError/503. 4 `test_t6200_concurrency.py` tests green in CI. No schema change, no migration. **TOP (found by T6190, 2026-07-28).** Same HAR: 4 requests issued within 4ms all take ~1460ms and complete within 2ms of each other — including `/api/health`, which touches no user data. Signature of a shared-resource wait (per-session/profile DB lock, single worker, or session middleware), not 4 independent latencies. If real it caps concurrency app-wide, not just project open. Scoped to **measure before fixing**; do T6190 first for a clean baseline. Also covers `playback-url`'s own 1.4s. |
| T6210 | [Update gate loops forever when the server is ahead but no newer bundle exists](tasks/T6210-update-gate-loops-without-newer-bundle.md) | 9 | 2 | 4.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-30 prod). Reported by user 2026-07-30: staging stuck in an unescapable "A new version is ready" modal - clicking Update now reloads and it returns. Tbug40p made the gate a pure truth comparison (`serverBuild > clientBuild`), which proves the SERVER moved but NOT that a newer CLIENT bundle exists. Both deploy workflows are path-filtered (deploy-backend.yml on `src/backend/**`, deploy-frontend.yml on `src/frontend/**`), so a backend-only commit raises the server number with no bundle published and the modal's exit condition ("reload onto a higher `__APP_BUILD__`") becomes unreachable - and it never auto-closes by design. MEASURED: staging bundle b130803b #3163 vs backend a442ad49 #3165, with `git diff --name-only b130803b..a442ad49 | grep -c "^src/frontend/"` = 0. LATENT ON PROD: prod escaped only because deploy_production.sh --all ships both halves from one commit (3166==3166); any `--backend-only` run would have stranded every prod user identically. Fixed in 65841559: gate only when a probe confirms a real waiting SW bundle (require `registration.waiting` specifically - a first-ever install activates with nothing to supersede and must not read as an update); throttled 5min + deduped since the failing state leaves the server permanently ahead while checkServerVersion runs on every API response. No probe registered (no SW / private mode) = NO gate, deliberately - those clients self-heal since with no precache any reload fetches the newest index.html. 8 new pins incl. the exact 3163/3165 repro; 1440 frontend tests pass; staging bundle verified at #3169. RESIDUAL: the SW path is jsdom-mocked, no real-browser test - watch the next frontend-only staging deploy to confirm the gate still fires PROMPTLY (over-correcting into never gating is not caught by unit tests). Follow-ups filed as T6220 (lockstep deploys) + T6230 (real-browser SW test). |
| T6220 | [Deploy both halves in lockstep so the build number means something](tasks/T6220-lockstep-deploy-build-number-accuracy.md) | 6 | 2 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **MERGED 2026-07-31** (master `4cdcc3e8`). Shipped a UNION `paths:` filter on both staging workflows rather than dropping filters (user decision): a commit touching EITHER half deploys BOTH; a docs-only commit deploys NEITHER, which keeps the numbers equal just as well with zero redundant CI. AC #1 rewritten to the real invariant (bundle build == backend build, never unequal). `deploy_production.sh` gate is ASYMMETRIC: `--frontend-only` stays allowed (bundle-ahead means serverBuild < clientBuild, which can never raise the gate), `--backend-only` refuses without `--accept-build-drift`. `generate-version.js` emits a served `/build.json`; `scripts/verify-build-lockstep.sh` polls both halves and fails loudly. **AC #2 PROVEN on the first real deploy**: the assertion rode out CDN propagation (attempt 1 rejected the SPA-catch-all HTML as unparseable), a 3193-vs-3165 mismatch while the backend deployed, and a Fly restart (curl timeouts), then confirmed `3193 == 3193` on attempt 10. Staging's prior drift is gone. Follow-up from T6210, explicitly scoped OUT of it. T6210 made the update loop structurally impossible (probe must confirm a waiting SW bundle), but did NOT align the deploys - so the build numbers still drift and `serverBuild > clientBuild` stays true for reasons unrelated to client staleness. Both staging workflows are path-filtered (`deploy-frontend.yml` on `src/frontend/**`, `deploy-backend.yml` on `src/backend/**`) and `deploy_production.sh` offers `--backend-only`/`--frontend-only`, so one half routinely ships alone. Not a stranding risk anymore - a SIGNAL-accuracy risk: the gate can fire late and any future logic trusting the build delta inherits the drift. Drop the `paths:` filters, make the single-half prod flags loud-or-gone, and assert bundle build == backend build after deploy so the invariant can't rot. Belt on top of T6210's braces - if this is dropped, T6210 must stay. |
| T6230 | [Update gate's ServiceWorker path has no real-browser test](tasks/T6230-update-gate-real-browser-sw-test.md) | 6 | 4 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **MERGED 2026-07-31** (master `4cdcc3e8`). Playwright spec drives a real Chromium ServiceWorker against two production builds served from one `node:http` origin (`e2e/helpers/staticBuildServer.js`); 4 cases, `npm run test:e2e:sw-gate`. NOT a `@staging-gate` member (owns its origin, gives no deployed-target signal); skips when `E2E_BASE_URL` is set. Zero production source changed - the 5-min throttle is bypassed by reloading (`lastProbeAt` resets per page load), never weakened. MUTATION-PROVEN both ways: forcing `probeForWaitingBundle` to `return false` turns case 2 RED (over-correction caught); dropping the `hasNewerBundle()` guard turns cases 1+3 RED (T6210 loop caught); both revert green. Ran 3x consecutively, zero flakes. NOTE: the task's premise that two same-commit builds differ on their own is FALSE - `version.json`'s buildTime is tree-shaken out, so builds are byte-identical; build B injects a deliberate marker asset and the spec asserts `swDiffers` in beforeAll so a regression to identical builds fails loud. (Also: the 8 jsdom pins below are actually 7.) Follow-up from T6210 - the residual risk it recorded. The gate DECISION is well pinned (8 jsdom tests incl. the 3163/3165 repro), but `probeForWaitingBundle`'s real `registration.update()`/`waiting`/`installing` behavior is MOCKED - same false-confidence class as T5380. Risk is OVER-correction, not the original loop: if the probe never resolves truthy in a real browser, the gate silently NEVER fires, users sit on a stale bundle forever, and no test goes red - invisible in CI by construction. Needs Playwright against a BUILT+served app (dev server registers no SW), serving two successive builds from one origin so `registration.waiting` is genuinely populated. Keep the jsdom pins - this adds a layer. INTERIM CHECK PASSED 2026-07-30: after the #3171 prod deploy, a browser holding the old #3166 SW got the prompt ONCE and it cleared permanently - so the probe does resolve truthy against a real `registration.waiting` and over-correction is ruled out for this path. Task stays open: that is one observation, not a regression guard - nothing goes red if the probe breaks later. Urgency dropped, scope unchanged. |
| T6300 | [My Reels tile actions are invisible until hover - user could not find them](tasks/T6300-reel-tile-hidden-actions.md) | 7 | 3 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **User report 2026-07-31.** "I cannot access the kabob or do anything (copy link, play, etc) with reels in myReels, the UI is gone." They disagree on BOTH axes: container max-w-2xl vs max-w-6xl 2xl:max-w-7xl (:777), and space-y-2 stack of p-3 rows vs grid-cols-2/sm:3/lg:6 of aspect-video GameTiles (:846, GameTile.jsx:189). | T6330 | [Video failures must log where the code looked](tasks/T6330-video-failure-diagnostics.md) | 6 | 2 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). **User direction 2026-08-01.** "In general, video failures should put more relevant data in the logs so we can easily check if the video exists where the code was looking for it." Triggered by a real incident: a game video would not load in Annotate on a cloned prod account. The FRONTEND diagnostics were excellent - `[VIDEO_DIAG] format_error status=401 content_type=application/json url=/api/games/2/video body={detail:Authentication required}` pinned it to a SESSION failure in one line. But answering "is the video actually where we think it is?" took three manual steps outside the app (read blake3_hash from the profile DB, probe R2 for `games/{hash}.mp4` across candidate prefixes, re-request with a fresh session to see the 302), because **the backend never logs the key it resolved**. Fix: on every video-serving failure path log `game_id/user_id/profile_id/blake3_hash`, the fully-qualified bucket+key, whether a failure-path HEAD found the object, and the outcome (`redirect_302`/`missing`/`denied`/`expired`) - so missing-object, denied and expired are distinguishable from logs alone. Sharpened by the env-key asymmetry: game videos are env-prefix-FREE (`games/{hash}.mp4`) while user media is env-prefixed, so "where did it look?" is genuinely non-obvious. Diagnostics ONLY - no new fallbacks (a missing object still fails loudly), never log the presigned URL (embeds credentials), and no new INFO logging on the success path (keeps presign off the hot path per T2880/T3380). Covers game/reel/working video + poster proxies. |
| T5920 | [R2 uploads can ship an under-checkpointed SQLite file at a bumped version (WAL)](tasks/T5920-wal-checkpoint-before-r2-upload.md) | 9 | 5 | 1.8 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). Found by the T4315 round-3 review; scoped OUT of that branch as systemic. R2 sync uploads the MAIN FILE ONLY; SQLite auto-checkpoints on LAST connection close, so if any other connection is open the upload ships bytes WITHOUT the recent commits - and bumps the version anyway. WORSE than not syncing: restore-if-newer (T4315) then sees local>=r2 and never re-pulls, CAS (T4310) trusts the stale copy, and any 'success' bookkeeping never retries. Reproduced: uploaded object had 0 games at db-version 1 while the share was marked materialized. `wal_checkpoint` exists ONLY in main.py's shutdown handler (2 sites); ~18 sync call sites are exposed, incl. the end-of-request middleware sync. NOTE a contended checkpoint returns busy=1 WITHOUT raising, so an unchecked call is a no-op - fix belongs in the upload primitive, must fail loudly on busy, and must not sit on the 30s busy_timeout (T4315 saw ~150s for a 5-recipient share). |
| T5910 | [Draft-tile action buttons don't appear on hover in a narrow desktop window](tasks/T5910-draft-tile-hover-actions-narrow-desktop.md) | 6 | 2 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). User report 2026-07-25: after resizing narrower (~478px), mousing over a Done reel draft reveals NO action buttons. Root cause: DraftTile.jsx:283-286 picks hover-vs-long-press from `useIsMobile()`, whose query is `(max-width: 1023px), ((hover: none) and (pointer: coarse))` - width OR touch. A narrow DESKTOP window matches on width, switching to the long-press path, but the long-press handlers are touch-only (:303-305), so a mouse user has NO way to reveal the actions. Fix: gate the MECHANISM on `useIsCoarsePointer()` (already exported, and its own comment documents this exact trap), not width - same lesson as T5360 "width != input type". Audit ProjectManager game cards + ReelTile for the same pattern. |

Verified live: relative URL = 200 text/html, API host = 200 image/jpeg with real bytes for all 6 games (incl. no-recap + expired). | T5870 | ["Your edits aren't saving" fires regularly; only a refresh clears it](tasks/T5870-edits-arent-saving-frequency-and-retry.md) | 8 | 5 | 1.6 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). **Bug 38p glitch 1** (the other two shipped 2026-07-25). Leads traced: `is_sync_failed()` literally returns `has_sync_pending()` (db_sync.py:250-252) and `.sync_pending` is set BEFORE the attempt (:154-157), so a merely IN-FLIGHT sync can tell the user their edits aren't saving - verify the `is_sync_attempt_in_progress` guard (:791) covers deferred/queued syncs. Sticky server marker explains refresh-only recovery (frontend `checkSyncStatus` already self-clears). `/api/retry-sync` was force-pushing + mis-reporting success pre-T4310. **Measure only on a build with T4310+T4315** - T4310 deliberately makes real conflicts MORE visible. | T5930 | ["New version" gate fires TWICE (before + after login) - plain reload strands the waiting SW](tasks/T5930-update-gate-fires-twice-stranded-sw.md) | 7 | 3 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-28 prod). User report on staging 2026-07-26 ("still hit with a double New version screen, before and after logging in; should run one time per account per new version"). Follow-up to bug39, whose fix IS deployed and did NOT close this. Verified before filing: staging frontend is built from 593224f9 which contains bug39's 1822fe64, no frontend changes on master since; staging backend STABLE at a4a28293 (8 consecutive identical X-App-Version samples, so not a flapping fleet); UpdateGateModal renders at main.jsx:65 outside auth so it does not remount across login - "twice" is two genuine requireUpdate events. Root cause hypothesis: runUpdate's version-mismatch branch (updateGateStore.js:84-85) does a PLAIN window.location.reload(), which does NOT skipWaiting - the old SW re-serves the old bundle, the new bundle stays waiting, and its onNeedRefresh then raises gate #2 around login. bug39's mitigation (letting 'sw' upgrade 'version-mismatch', :29-39) is RACE-DEPENDENT and its own comment states the assumption ("by the time the user reads the modal and clicks, onNeedRefresh has fired") - click before the SW installs and the upgrade never lands. Fix: ask registration.waiting directly at click time (pwaUpdate.js already does this in onReturnToApp) instead of trusting the callback race. Keep the flush barrier + logged-out skip. Frontend-only. |

| T5390 | [Overlay spotlight circle can't be moved/resized by touch on mobile](tasks/T5390-overlay-circle-touch-select-then-manipulate.md) | 7 | 4 | 1.8 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod; interaction model superseded by T5570 bounding-box redesign). User report 2026-07-18 (imankh, staging smoke, mobile): in Overlay mode the spotlight CIRCLE can't be moved/resized by touch. Root cause (code read): HighlightOverlay.jsx wires MOUSE-ONLY handlers (handleEllipseMouseDown/handleResizeMouseDown/handleMouseMove) with no touch/pointer handlers, so a touch relies on synthesized mouse events and LOSES to the video's tap/swipe (play/seek) zones under the same area. Pre-existing (NOT a T4550 regression - T4550 only changed the coordinate math). Fix (user-directed, mobile-only): select-then-manipulate - one TAP selects the circle (ephemeral state) -> video tap-nav suppressed over the circle, resize handles/levers appear (>=44px, pairs with T5360), body drags to move + handles drag to resize; tap elsewhere deselects. Implement via Pointer Events + setPointerCapture (one path for mouse+touch), reuse T4550 useVideoDisplayRect screenToVideo. Desktop byte-identical (no selection step). Frontend-only, M-tier, no schema/backend/persistence. Same mobile cluster as T5290/T5360/T5370/T5380. |
| T5330 | [Email game-share recipient skips the new-user flow](tasks/T5330-share-recipient-skips-new-user-flow.md) | 7 | 4 | 1.8 | DONE | [x] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod). User report 2026-07-17: a user who signs up after being emailed a game share does NOT get the new-user flow (NUF) onboarding -- exactly the primary viral-acquisition cohort. Root cause traced end-to-end (code read): quest_1 onboarding steps are DB-derived (quests.py:171-184 `upload_game`/`add_clip`/`rate_clip`/`annotate_brilliant` via `rc.total`/`rc.reels` backfills), and the T3230 auto-materialize block (session_init.py:162-202) copies the shared game+clips into the brand-new profile BEFORE the first quests/progress call -- plus materialize_game_share auto-creates a draft reel per 5-star clip (materialization.py:503-512), so `rc.reels>=1` -> quest_1 reads complete and questStore progressive disclosure advances the recipient past onboarding. Decided rule (user): being shared a game must be INVISIBLE to NUF progress -- not started -> gets full NUF; mid-NUF -> keeps own earned progress unchanged; already done -> unchanged. Approach: quest_1 DB-derived counts exclude shared-in content by provenance (clips already carry `shared_by`; add markers on the copied game + auto-reel), keep claimed-quest self-heal + own-content backfills intact so established users aren't re-onboarded. Short Architect pass to lock the provenance mechanism + migration need. Related: T3230, T2915, T3290 (returning-user NUF), T4910 (link-share path). Backend-first, M-tier. |
| T5370 | [Spotlight loop playback: primary "Play spotlight" loops the region, de-emphasized "Play full"](tasks/T5370-spotlight-loop-playback.md) | 7 | 4 | 1.8 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod). User report 2026-07-17 (imankh): while adding a spotlight in Overlay mode, users lose their place when the playhead runs past the spotlight section (linear play-to-end only). Make the PRIMARY play button loop the spotlight + add a DE-EMPHASIZED secondary "Play full" button + always-easy return to the spotlight start. Locked product decisions (user): (1) loop scope = SPAN of ALL regions `[min(startTime), max(endTime)]` (one continuous window, not per-region/selected-only); (2) return-to-start = BOTH the primary loop button seeks-to-start-and-loops AND a "Back to spotlight" pill appears once the playhead is past the span. Zero regions -> primary = plain Play/Pause, no secondary/pill. Design: overlay-local `useSpotlightLoop` hook (wrap seek at span end; NOT added to shared `useVideo` — keeps Annotate/Framing mode-agnostic) + ephemeral `spotlightPlayMode` view state (NEVER persisted; the reactive `seek()` is playback control, not a DB/store write, so not a T350-class violation) + optional `isLooping`/`secondaryPlay` props on shared `Controls` (other modes byte-identical). Flows to mobile via the same `<Controls>`; buttons/pill >=44px (pairs with T5360). Frontend-only, M-tier, no schema/backend/persistence. |
| T5360 | [Framing controls have sub-standard touch targets on tablet (26px)](tasks/T5360-touch-target-sizes-tablet.md) | 6 | 3 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod). User report 2026-07-17 (imankh, tablet): Framing control buttons too small to reliably tap. Root cause (code read): shared `Button` primitive's `sm` iconOnly size uses `min-w-11 min-h-11 sm:min-w-0 sm:min-h-0` — the 44px touch floor is stripped at the `sm` WIDTH breakpoint (>=640px), but a tablet is >=640px AND touch, so it lands in the "desktop" bucket and collapses to ~26px (14px icon + p-1.5). Width != input type is the bug. Hits play/pause, step, restart, fullscreen, zoom, mobile Crop toggle (all shared `size="sm" iconOnly`) + the hand-rolled keyframe delete/copy (~25px) and diamond hit area (~36px). 26px fails Apple HIG/Material/WCAG-AAA (44/48), barely clears WCAG-AA (24). Fix: pointer-capability Tailwind variants (`coarse-pointer`/`fine-pointer` via addVariant) + unified iconOnly 44px floor on coarse pointers in Button.jsx + KeyframeMarker hand-rolled bump; DESKTOP BYTE-IDENTICAL (only touch >=640px moves 26->44). Extends T4930's usability matrix with a 4th `assertTouchTargetSizes` invariant (runs on the iPad project). Frontend-only, M-tier, no schema/backend/persistence. |
| T5350 | [Clip-gesture sync_failed 503 confusing/invisible on frontend](tasks/T5350-clip-sync-failed-frontend-ux.md) | 5 | 3 | 1.7 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod). Found by T4320 reviewer (MAJOR, frontend, deferred). T4320 made clip saves durable -> backend now returns retryable 503 {code:sync_failed} on failure, but the clip 503 reuses the "your reel was not moved" copy (wrong for a clip) and useRawClipSave.js doesn't handle code===sync_failed to offer Retry/not-saved like publish/move do. So T4320's "never a silent success" is only half-delivered on the failure path. Give clip gestures a clip-appropriate message + Retry handling (update/delete too); no silent success. Completes T4320's user-visibility criterion. Frontend-only, M-tier. |
| T5380 | [CropOverlay drops the first drag gesture after mount](tasks/T5380-cropoverlay-first-drag-dropped.md) | 5 | 2 | 2.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-20 prod). **RE-FIXED (T5380b, landed 2026-07-19, STAGING):** the first fix passed only a jsdom test and did NOT work on the real deployed browser. Real root cause found via a real-chromium harness: the detailed `VideoLoadingOverlay` was missing `pointer-events-none`, so while the video buffers its z-40 overlay sat over the always-interactive crop/highlight reticule and swallowed the FIRST mousedown (unmounts when buffering ends -> 2nd+ drags work). Fix = one class on `VideoLoadingOverlay.jsx` (also fixes Overlay highlight first-drag); CropOverlay/useVideoDisplayRect untouched. Real-browser proof: pre-fix first {0,0}, post-fix first {-40,-30}. Original context: Found by the T5320 worker proving T4550 on staging. The FIRST crop-adjust drag after CropOverlay mounts is silently dropped (crop doesn't move); 2nd+ drags work. Root cause: window mousemove/mouseup listeners attach in a useEffect gated on isDragging, so the first mousemove fires before the effect commits the listeners. User impact: opening Framing and immediately dragging the crop does nothing until you release+redrag — friction on a core gesture. Fix: attach listeners on mousedown (or unconditionally + gate the handler bodies), minimal, don't touch useVideoDisplayRect; check HighlightOverlay/PlayerDetectionOverlay for the same race; then remove T4550 spec's warm-up prime so the first drag is the regression proof. Frontend-only, S/M-tier. |

| T5630 | [Staged, resumable, unified export finalize (recovery == normal export)](tasks/T5630-resumable-unified-export-finalize.md) | 5 | 8 | 0.6 | DONE | [x] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-22 prod, profile_db v028 migrated staging+prod). DESIGN-GATED, architecture written (see task). Root cause of Brilliant Control's empty overlay data: export interrupted by a server restart, finalized via the recovery-only `finalize_modal_export` which skips detection + never writes highlights_data (normal export builds full overlay data in-band). The two finalize paths diverged, and the pipeline has NO per-stage checkpoint so recovery can't complete only missing work. Fix: add export_jobs.stage + persist output_key (profile_db v028) so recovery resumes only missing stages; extract ONE `finalize_export(job, output_key, user_id)` (detect via reconstructed source_clips from input_data -> highlights_data + detections_data, default-region fallback) called by BOTH in-band and recovery. | T6610 | [Overlay text elements: drag to reposition, and a delete button you can hit](tasks/player-intro/T6610-overlay-text-element-manipulation.md) | 6 | 3 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-13 prod). **Merged 2780032d.** Worker VERIFIED the current state first (existing T5225 lever spec 10/10) and confirmed the task's premise: multi-block, click-to-add, delete, select-drives-editor and lever resize all already worked, so only the body drag was built - no rebuild. moveTextBlock moves both edges together (duration preserved, clamp-only), leading-edge snapping reuses snapToBoundary/SNAP_PX, arrow-key nudge for a11y. Delete padding: lane h-14->h-28 with getTotalLayerHeight/label in lockstep so controls clear the scrollbar, 44px coarse hit box. **Declared limitation:** 375px screenshots captured but no-horizontal-overflow NOT asserted, because the textdiag harness is fixed-1000px and asserting there would measure the harness not the component (correct call - this is the failing-open harness class); change is vertical-only. **User request 2026-08-05 with screenshots.** Expectation is add / remove / drag-to-reposition text elements in a text region, with Edit Text acting on the SELECTED element. **Most of that already works and must NOT be rebuilt** - multiple blocks, click-to-add (OverlayScreen.jsx:947), delete, selection driving the editor, and lever resize with boundary snapping all exist. The REAL gap is that only the two levers drag: grabbing the block BODY just selects it, so moving an element in time means dragging one edge then the other, changing its duration on the way. Add a body drag that moves start+end together preserving duration, reusing the existing computeSnappedTime/SNAP_PX mechanics (T5225 says explicitly this is not a new drag idiom), leading edge snaps, clamped to the region, lever-vs-body hit-testing preserved, ONE surgical write on drag END extending the existing onMoveTextStart/End path (no second write path, no useEffect). | T6650 | [Deleting an intro card destroys the profile's intro photo](tasks/player-intro/T6650-card-delete-destroys-profile-intro-photo.md) | 7 | 3 | 2.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-13 prod). **Merged 2bc2fb86 2026-08-10** (verified live via Playwright against a fresh container, real repro account imankh@gmail.com/9fa7378c: created a card defaulting to the profile's intro photo key, deleted it, confirmed the profile photo object still resolves — 200, same key — both via the Edit Profile UI and a direct R2 fetch). **User-hit data loss 2026-08-07, root-caused by the supervisor.** One R2 object, two owners: `introCardDefaults.js:57` defaults a new card's `image_key` to the profile's `introPhotoKey` (the SAME key, by epic decision 3b, test-pinned), and `intro_cards.py:331-333` hard-deletes that object when the card is deleted - while `user_settings.intro_photo_key` still points at it. Create a card, delete it, and the profile photo is silently destroyed; the Edit Profile thumb breaks and a presigned GET 404s. Verified live: key persisted for `66b98be0...png`, full paginated R2 listing under the profile's `intro/` prefix returns 6 objects ALL dated 2026-08-04 - today's object is gone. Contradicts the ownership contract asserted in `profiles.py:301-316` ("this endpoint remains the sole owner of the R2 object"). **The handoff's upload-ordering theory is REFUTED and must not be re-derived**: `store_intro_image` runs before `set_intro_photo_key`, returns None on failure (500), and `retry_r2_call` re-raises when retries exhaust - a failed PUT cannot produce a persisted key. Fix is ownership, not ordering: either the card delete checks for other referencing cards + the profile key before deleting (recommended, no schema change), or defaulting COPIES the object to a card-scoped key. Must not weaken T5230's compliance purge, which deletes intentionally. Dangling keys need a visible "photo missing" state, and repair CLEARS the key - never re-points it at the surviving 2026-08-04 photo, which is a different image. **SECOND HALF confirmed by the user the same morning:** they re-uploaded (object landed 05:39:30Z, verified) and the CARD still showed broken - cards store a SNAPSHOT of the key (`introCardDefaults.js:57`, `IntroCardRail.jsx:372`), so a profile re-upload never propagates, and the card renders a bare `<img>` with no missing-object state. | T6600 | [Draft tiles paint over the intro-card modal - nested stacking contexts and an ad-hoc z-index scale](tasks/player-intro/T6600-modal-z-order-and-stacking-scale.md) | 7 | 4 | 1.8 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-13 prod). **Merged b6878608.** **Split out of T6580 item 1, 2026-08-05, after a second screenshot round.** Draft/reel tiles render ON TOP of the Intro cards modal - over the panel, not through the backdrop; the tell is crisp UNDIMMED cyan buttons, which content behind an 80% scrim cannot be. **Misdiagnosed twice as bleed-through and "fixed" with a heavier scrim** (T6540 /70->/80, T6580 /80->/90+blur); neither could work because the elements paint ABOVE the scrim. T6540 explicitly left the question open and this settles it: a layering bug. Cause is two facts combining - (1) ManageProfilesModal's root is fixed z-50, which ESTABLISHES a stacking context, and IntroCardsModal renders as a CHILD of that subtree with its own z-50 (ManageProfilesModal.jsx:302/:451, IntroCardsModal.jsx:88), so its root-level ceiling is z-50 whatever number it uses; (2) DraftTile portals overlays TO document.body at z-[60]/z-[70] (DraftTile.jsx:708,711), deliberately, because its hover:scale transform (:411) breaks fixed descendants. Tiles outrank the modal BY CONSTRUCTION. Fix = portal/hoist the modal out of the parent context PLUS one ordered z-index scale in a single module (values are ad hoc across ~30 components: z-40/50/[60]/[70]/[90]); nested modals must be expressible (ManageProfiles->IntroCards->ConfirmationDialog). Bumping IntroCardsModal alone is REJECTED - it moves the collision to CollectionPlayer z-[70] and LockedReasonModal z-[90]. SWEEP for other collidable pairs; three wrong diagnoses means the ordering is not inspectable by reading. Verify WITH A TILE HOVERED (hover is what portals - an unhovered shot falsely passes, the failing-open harness class). |

| T6250 | ↳ [Entering Overlay fires overlay-data 3x and outdated-clips 2x](tasks/post-har-latency/T6250-overlay-entry-duplicate-fetches.md) | 6 | 3 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). Epic 2/6. Framing->Overlay on project 30 fires 5 requests inside 11ms where 2 would do: outdated-clips at t=44352 + 44355, overlay-data at t=44354 + 44356 + 44363. Same defect class T6190 fixed for project-open (two owners fetching the same data), on a transition T6190 did not cover. **overlay-data x3 cannot be StrictMode alone** - dev double-invoke gives EVEN multiples, so an odd count proves >=2 genuine owners. outdated-clips x2 may be StrictMode; verify against a production build before touching it. Unlike T6190 sequential ~685ms-apart pair these are 1-9ms apart and genuinely concurrent, so an in-flight latch would collapse them - there is not one. One owner per fetch; do NOT add a blanket request cache. |
| T6260 | ↳ [Read endpoints send no-cache with no validator - nothing can ever 304](tasks/post-har-latency/T6260-etag-304-read-endpoints.md) | 6 | 4 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). Epic 3/6. All 14 JSON read endpoints audited in the HAR return `private, no-cache, stale-while-revalidate` with NO ETag and NO Last-Modified - so the browser can never send If-None-Match, the server can never answer 304, and every request re-sends the full body. stale-while-revalidate is inert without a validator. The stack already does this correctly for poster images (max-age=86400 + ETag), which is the proof the pattern works here; the JSON endpoints just never got it. Add ETags starting with the boot set (projects/games/downloads/settings/profiles). Do NOT add max-age to these - they are mutable per-user data and a stale window would show users their own edits disappearing; 304-on-revalidate is the win. Reuse the existing poster ETag helper, do not write a second one. |
| T6270 | ↳ [Every achievement POST is chased by a quests/progress GET](tasks/post-har-latency/T6270-achievement-post-progress-roundtrip.md) | 4 | 2 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). Epic 4/6. /api/quests/progress fetched 4x per session, and 3 of them follow an achievement POST by 100-1400ms (returned_home t=22202->23602, opened_framing_editor t=37514->37619, opened_overlay_editor t=44311->44391). The POST already mutates progress server-side, so returning updated progress in its own response body removes the follow-up GET entirely. Note the opened_framing_editor one lands on the project-open critical path - the exact window T6190 was clearing. Keep the standalone GET (boot needs it). Check first whether callers await the POST at all - some achievement posts are fire-and-forget, and there the right fix is dropping the follow-up GET rather than enriching the response. |
| T6280 | ↳ [Small double-fires - rank/confidence x2, games/{id}/video 302 x2](tasks/post-har-latency/T6280-small-double-fires.md) | 3 | 2 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). Epic 5/6, lowest impact. rank/confidence fires twice at the IDENTICAL millisecond (t=87807, 142ms each) - two subscribers or an unguarded effect, not a double click. games/6/video 302s twice (t=54756, 54767) with games/6/load between them, each paying a redirect; worth checking because a duplicated 302 on a media URL can mean two src assignments = two connection setups. **Both are x2, so both may be dev-only StrictMode** - capture against a production build FIRST; "StrictMode only, no action" written up is a successful close. Keep the fix proportional; if the cause is structural, file it rather than forcing a fix. |
| T6290 | ↳ [10 posters fire at once during boot and compete with it](tasks/post-har-latency/T6290-poster-first-load-batch.md) | 4 | 3 | 1.3 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-29 prod). Epic 6/6. 25 poster images per session, 60-78KB each; the first 10 all start at t=22278 (the instant the boot storm releases) and take 1.8-2.3s each, exceeding the browser ~6-connection HTTP/1.1 limit. **Caching is already correct and is NOT the problem** - posters send max-age=86400 + ETag, so repeat visits are cheap; this is first-load only. Re-measure AFTER T6240: these durations are contaminated by the 22s backend stall and may substantially resolve with it. Then check how many of the 10 were actually on screen (ReelTile already uses loading="lazy"; does the Drafts batch?) and confirm HTTP/2 at the edge via T2540 rather than re-deriving it. Do not add a service-worker or bespoke image cache - the HTTP cache is doing its job. |

| T5710 | ↳ [Per-layer recaps (Team / Athlete)](tasks/team-game-share/T5710-per-layer-recaps.md) | 6 | 4 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). Epic 2/5. `_get_annotated_clips` + `_generate_recap` + `recap-data` gain a layer filter; Team Recap at `recaps/{id}_team.mp4` (+poster), athlete recap keeps the existing key; RecapPlayerModal splits into Team Recap / {Athlete} Recap with player-tag filter chips; combined recap retired. `ensure_recap(game_id, layer)` helper for T5720's stitch-on-share. Prefer derived R2 keys over a schema change. **WIP 2026-07-31** (container worker, wave 1 alongside T5700; branch `feature/T5710-per-layer-recaps`). Design gate PASSED + user-approved, doc `tasks/team-game-share/T5710-design.md`. Locked: derived R2 keys confirmed (NO column, NO migration) — every game ends with exactly 2 layer-pure recaps, `recaps/{id}.mp4` (athlete, existing key/consumers unchanged) + `recaps/{id}_team.mp4`. Legacy discriminator = the frozen mapping json is WRAPPED `{schema, layer, clips}`; a bare list = pre-T5710 = mixed-layer. Core rule: a legacy MIXED recap is NEVER served as a stitched per-layer recap — only as a seek source with a layer-filtered rail (reuses the existing game-video seek-through mechanism), so nothing is ever mislabeled and `recap-data` stays read-only (no stitch on GET). User decision 2026-07-31: BUILD the seek-filtered legacy path — an expired game must stay watchable, and post-expiry the mixed mp4 is the only surviving artifact; `ensure_recap` SLICES the 2 layer-pure mp4s out of it (no game source needed) when a gesture demands real files; mixed mp4 present but mapping json missing/unresolvable → explicit single combined entry playing the whole file, never under a per-layer label. Team recap co-generates in the AUTO-EXPORT path only; T5720's on-demand `ensure_recap` stays single-layer. **T4140 landmine caught at design:** overwriting `.mp4` athlete-pure destroys the TEAM clips' re-edit source, so `_team.mp4` is written BEFORE `.mp4` is overwritten and `_resolve_recap_source` must search both. |

| T5745 | ↳ [Layer-aware import merge (My Athlete clips survive a claim)](tasks/team-game-share/T5745-layer-aware-import-merge.md) | 8 | 2 | 4.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-08-03 prod). Share the Game follow-up bug. `_materialize_clips` merged an incoming clip with ANY intersecting existing clip regardless of layer and hardcoded `my_athlete=0` onto the survivor — so a claim (Team clips imported onto plays the owner tags for their own athlete; same play = guaranteed intersection) silently converted the recipient's My Athlete clip to Team, stretched its bounds, and swallowed the team clip. Fix: `_get_existing_clips` SELECTs `my_athlete`; new `_is_team_layer` (`== 0`, NULL = My Athlete); merge only same-layer (incoming Team ↔ existing Team), cross-layer intersection becomes a plain insert so both coexist; UPDATE no longer touches `my_athlete`. Behavior-only, no schema/migration. Same-layer Team-vs-Team merge still dedupes (re-claim idempotency preserved). |

Audit: `total_usage_seconds` is a single Postgres running total (2 write sites in analytics.py); NO per-user time buckets exist (`daily_counters` is per-origin). Avg/wk = DERIVED at read (total / weeks-since-signup, min 1wk clamp — no new state). Last-7d = new `user_usage_daily(user_id, day, seconds)` table (postgres migration v019 + _SCHEMA_DDL), upserted via ONE shared add_usage_seconds helper at both existing write sites, same transaction; grouped SUM join in admin query (no N+1). No backfill possible — Last-7d is honest-partial for the first week after deploy. Check impersonation guard consistency. Migration agent yes; manual migrate trigger per env post-deploy. |

Leaves the step-5 migration seam clean for T5080. L-tier, Architect gate. |

| T5270 | ↳ [Warm recap poster at share creation](tasks/clearest-frame-posters/T5270-warm-recap-poster-at-share-creation.md) | 6 | 2 | 3.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-16 prod). | T5340 | [sync_db_to_r2_explicit uses ContextVar for R2 key (cross-profile corruption)](tasks/T5340-sync-explicit-contextvar-key-bug.md) | 8 | 4 | 2.0 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-18 prod). Found by T4320 impl + reviewer (independently). sync_db_to_r2_explicit(user,profile) is documented ContextVar-independent but its R2 KEY comes from r2_key -> get_current_profile_id() (ContextVar), while the LOCAL file uses the arg. When they disagree it uploads the right DB to the WRONG profile's R2 key. CONFIRMED live on T4850 "Transfer Reels" (DONE/prod): move_reels_to_profile syncs target_profile_id without setting the ContextVar to target -> target DB overwrites the SOURCE profile's R2 copy (corruption) and the target never gets the moved reels. Fix the PRIMITIVE (key from arg, not ContextVar) so all callers are safe; audit every caller; prod scan for existing corruption + repair. No silent fallback. Data-integrity depth. Durability campaign. |
| T5300 | [T4120 durability test: forced sync fault returns 202, never surfaces sync_failed](tasks/T5300-t4120-durability-fault-not-surfaced.md) | 6 | 4 | 1.5 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-18 prod). Found by T4934 worker (out-of-scope, pre-existing). e2e/T4120-self-verify-durability.spec.js fails locally with AND without T4934 (stash-proven): forced /api/test/sync-fault + POST /api/export/render-overlay returns HTTP 202 and the terminal event never carries code=sync_failed (assertion ~line 105). Two possibilities to determine: (1) REAL durability gap — the 202 queued/Modal render path bypasses the T4110 durable boundary that only the synchronous copy paths honor (overlay.py:2043/:2110), so a sync failure there is silently swallowed (COMPLETE announced without the R2 write landing) — a genuine data-safety hole; or (2) test routing assumption (container routes render to the queued path, test assumed synchronous). Fix at the source (durable boundary on the queued path) or fix the test to exercise the real boundary — NO weakened assertion, no silent skip. Backend + 1 e2e spec; investigation gates scope (M, L if real gap). |
| T5320 | [Staging e2e unusable: seed real fixtures + shorten deployed timeout](tasks/T5320-staging-e2e-fixtures-and-timeout.md) | 6 | 5 | 1.2 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-18 prod). Confirmed by a live staging characterization run 2026-07-17 (43 non-seam specs; stopped at 39/112 once conclusive: 14 pass / 20 fail, 10 were 5.0m timeouts). The "73 failed / 4.3h" was NOT the /api/test/* seams (only 2 specs, handled by T4934) — it's that staging's e2e@test.local has NO seeded data, so every real-workflow UI spec (game-loading, keyframe-integrity, full-workflow, annotate-game-clock) hangs to the 5-min per-test timeout. Fix: (1) seed a documented fixture (game+clips+framed project+published reel) into the staging test account idempotently (copy_user_between_envs.py or a prod-guarded seed script; loginAsRealUser is already staging-capable) — the actual unlock; (2) short deployed-target per-test timeout (reuse T4934's E2E_TIMEOUT_MS, ~45-60s) so the suite finishes in minutes and real regressions read as fast specific failures. Also triage residual non-data failures (derisk-staging-*.qa, blob-url dynamic-import, collections 360px) individually. Builds on T4934 (merge first). M/L-tier. |
| T4934 | [E2E suite has no staging-compatible mode](tasks/T4934-e2e-suite-not-staging-compatible.md) | 6 | 5 | 1.2 | DONE | [ ] | DONE (deployed 2026-07-18 prod). Found running the full suite against staging as a 2026-07-16 pre-deploy derisk pass: 73 failed / 18 skipped / 29 passed over 4.3h, but the failure shape is systemic not 73 bugs — most specs call `/api/test/*` seams in setup (sync-fault etc.), which aren't mounted on staging (`storage.py` `_test_seams_enabled`), so setup hangs on an HTML-not-JSON response until the 5-minute timeout; a near-contiguous wall of timeouts from full-workflow.spec.js onward matches this exactly, while pure API-integration tests (no UI setup) passed clean. screen-usability.spec.js failures are T4930's already-documented emulation blind spot, not new. Net: the suite can't currently be trusted as a staging derisk gate — a real regression and this seam-gap look identical (both time out). Fix = either a staging-safe seam substitute or tag+skip seam-dependent specs when E2E_BASE_URL points at a deployed target (playwright.config.js already has the env-based target switch to hang this off of), plus re-time the suite once seam-hangs are gone (4.3h is impractical as a gate regardless). No silent fallback — the gap must be visible (explicit skip+reason) or closed, never papered over. |
